which do not occur in
xv^{-1}_{\mu}
.

(b)

The factor
s_{j-1}
creates an inversion if
y(j)>y(a)
and removes an inversion if

y(j)<y(a)
.

(c)

The factor
(s_{j}\cdots s_{v_{\mu}(j)-1})

undoes inversions
yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)
for
k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}
,

adds inversions
yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)>yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)
for
k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}
,

(d)

The factor
(s_{v_{\mu}(j)-1}s_{v_{\mu}(j)-2}\cdots s_{j})

undoes inversions
yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)>yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))
for
k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}
,

adds inversions
yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))
for
k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}
,

Thus, if
yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))>yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)
(so that
y(j)>y(a)
) then

\displaystyle\ell(yc^{-1}_{a}c_{j}v^{-1}_{\mu})

\displaystyle-\ell(yv^{-1}_{\mu})-\ell(c^{-1}_{a}c_{j})=\ell(yc^{-1}_{a}c_{j}v^{-1}_{\mu})-\ell(yv^{-1}_{\mu})-(j-a)

\displaystyle=(j-1-a)+1

\displaystyle\qquad-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)>yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)\})

\displaystyle\qquad-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))>yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)\})

\displaystyle\qquad-(j-a)

\displaystyle=-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)\})

\displaystyle\qquad-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\})

\displaystyle=2\cdot(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\}).

Then, if
yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)
(so that
y(j)<y(a)
) then

\displaystyle\ell(yc^{-1}_{a}c_{j}v^{-1}_{\mu})

\displaystyle-\ell(yv^{-1}_{\mu})-\ell(c^{-1}_{a}c_{j})=\ell(yc^{-1}_{a}c_{j}v^{-1}_{\mu})-\ell(yv^{-1}_{\mu})-(j-a)

\displaystyle=(j-1-a)-1

\displaystyle\qquad-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)\})

\displaystyle\qquad-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\})

\displaystyle\qquad-(j-a)

\displaystyle=-1-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)\})

\displaystyle\qquad-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)\})

\displaystyle\qquad-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)\})

\displaystyle\qquad-(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)\})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)\})

\displaystyle=-2-2\cdot(\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\}).

If
y(j)>y(a)
then

\displaystyle\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}(\ell(yc^{-1}_{a}c_{j}v^{-1}_{\mu})

\displaystyle-\ell(yv^{-1}_{\mu})-\ell(c^{-1}_{a}c_{j}))

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\}

\displaystyle=\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ y(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<y(j)\}

\displaystyle=\#\{v_{\mu}(b)\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ y(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(b))<y(j)\}

\displaystyle=\#\{b\in\{v^{-1}_{\mu}(j),\ldots,v^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j)-1)\}\ |\ y(a)<y(b)<y(j)\}

and,
if
y(j)<y(a)
then

\displaystyle(v_{\mu}(j)

\displaystyle-(j-1))+\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\big(\ell(yc^{-1}_{a}c_{j}v^{-1}_{\mu})-\ell(yv^{-1}_{\mu})-\ell(c^{-1}_{a}c_{j})\big)

\displaystyle=(v_{\mu}(j)-j+1)-1-\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\}

\displaystyle=((v_{\mu}(j)-1)-(j-1))-\#\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ |\ yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))\}

\displaystyle=\#\Big\{k\in\{j,\ldots,v_{\mu}(j)-1\}\ \Big|\ \begin{array}[]{l}\hbox{$yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))$}\\
\hbox{ or $yv^{-1}_{\mu}(a)<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j))<yv^{-1}_{\mu}(k)$}\end{array}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\#\Big\{b\in\{v^{-1}_{\mu}(j),\ldots,v^{-1}_{\mu}(v_{\mu}(j)-1)\}\ \Big|\ \begin{array}[]{l}\hbox{$y(b)<y(a)<y(j)$}\\
\hbox{ or $y(a)<y(j)<y(b)$}\end{array}\Big\}.

These last two expressions are exactly the numbers of coninversion triples that appear
in
[
HHL06
, Lemma 3.6.3]
for the box
(j,1)
filled with
y(a)
in a filling of shape

\mu
with basement
(y(1),\ldots,y(n))
.

5
Type
GL_{n}
DAArt, DAHA and the polynomial representation

The power tools that enable us to
construct and manipulate Macdonald polynomials with ease
are the polynomial generators
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
,
the Cherednik-Dunkl operators
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
and the intertwiners

\tau^{\vee}_{1},\ldots,\tau^{\vee}_{n-1},\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}
which all live inside the
double affine Hecke algebra

\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}
. In this section we will build the Macdonald polynomials
E_{\mu}
by
first constructing the double affine Artin group
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
,
then the elements
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
and
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
,
then the DAHA
\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}
and
the intertwiners
\tau^{\vee}_{1},\ldots,\tau^{\vee}_{n-1},\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}
.
Let us begin by defining the DAArt
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
and establishing its primary dualities.
The definition is by generators and relations and the dualities are automorphisms of

\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
. The double affine Hecke algebra
\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}

is constructed as a quotient of the
group algebra of
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
by the Hecke relations
T_{i}^{2}=(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})T_{i}+1
.

Use Coxeter diagram shorthand for relations so that

a
b

indicates
aba=bab
, and
a
b

indicates
ab=ba
,

5.1
The type
GL_{n}
double affine Artin group (DAArt)

The element
q
will be a parameter in the Macdonald polynomials. In the definition
of the DAArt by generators and relations the elememt
q

appears as a central element of the group, but in Section
5.6

the element
q
will get specialized to be a complex parameter.

The
type
GL_{n}
double affine Artin group (DAArt)

\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}

is generated by
q,g^{\vee},g,S_{0}^{\vee},S_{0},T_{1},\ldots,T_{n-1}

with the relations

{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 42.76 0 L 75.35 0 M 160.87 0 L 193.46 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}{{}}{{}}{{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{{}}}{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}
}{{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{{}}}{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}
}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{}{}{}{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}}{{}}{{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{{}}}{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}
}{{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{{}}}{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}
}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{}{}{}{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 41.91 2.25 C 65.77 23.41 83.54 32.3 114.79 38.69 M 121.43 38.69 C 152.68 32.3 170.45 23.41 194.31 2.25}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}{{}}
{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 82.13 0 L 154.09 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{matrix}\quad&\begin{array}[]{c}g^{\vee}S_{0}^{\vee}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=T_{1},\\
\\
g^{\vee}T_{i}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=T_{i+1},\qquad g^{\vee}T_{n-1}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=S^{\vee}_{0},\end{array}\end{matrix}

(5.1)

q\in Z(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}})\qquad\hbox{and}

T_{1}g^{\vee}g=gg^{\vee}T_{n-1}^{-1}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}g(g^{\vee})^{-1}=q(g^{\vee})^{-1}gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}.

(5.2)

for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-2\}
.

The two visible symmetries in this defnition, switching the Coxeter diagram containing
S_{0}
and
the Coxeter diagram containing
S_{0}^{\vee}
, and flipping the Coxeter diagrams about the middle,
form two important dualities. These dualities are expressed as involutive automorphisms
of the DAArt
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
.

Theorem 5.1
.

(
\vee
Duality)
There is an involution
\iota\colon\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
with

\iota(q)=q^{-1},\qquad\iota(T_{i})=T_{i}^{-1},\qquad\iota(S_{0}^{\vee})=S_{0}^{-1},\qquad\iota(g)=g^{\vee}.

(
\vdash\dashv
Duality) There is an involution
\eta\colon\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}\to\tilde{B}_{GL_{n}}
with

\eta(q)=q,\qquad\eta(T_{i})=T_{n-i},\qquad\eta(g)=g^{-1},\qquad\eta(g^{\vee})=(g^{\vee})^{-1}.

Proof.

(a) Applying
\iota
to the relations in (
5.1
)
switches the upper (nonchecked) relations with the lower (checked) relations.
Applying
\iota
to the relations in (
5.2
)
produces the relations
q^{-1}\in Z(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}})
,

T_{1}^{-1}gg^{\vee}=g^{\vee}gT_{n-1}\quad\hbox{and}\quad T_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}g^{\vee}g^{-1}=q^{-1}g^{-1}g^{\vee}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1},

respectively.
Thus the relations in (
5.2
) are preserved under
\iota
.

(b) The involution
\eta
preserves the relations in (
5.1
).
Applying
\eta
to the relations in (
5.2
)
produces the relations
q\in Z(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}})
,

T_{n-1}(g^{\vee})^{-1}g^{-1}=g^{-1}(g^{\vee})^{-1}T_{1}^{-1}\quad\hbox{and}\quad T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}g^{-1}g^{\vee}=qg^{\vee}g^{-1}T_{1}\cdots T_{n-1}

which are equivalent to the original relations in (
5.2
)
by taking inverses.
∎

5.2
The elements
X^{\varepsilon_{1}},\ldots,X^{\varepsilon_{n}}

and
Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}},\ldots,Y^{\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}

The elements
X^{\varepsilon_{1}},\ldots,X^{\varepsilon_{n}}
will be used as the
generators for a polynomial ring (inside the group algebra of
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
),
and the Macdonald polynomials are polynomials in these variables. Inside the DAArt,
these elements form a large commuttative subgroups and, because of duality,
there is
another
large commutative subgroup generated by elements

Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{1}},\ldots,Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{n}}
.
In this section we define these elements and give alternate presentations of

\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{n}
in terms of these elements.

Define
Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}},\ldots,Y^{\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}

and
X^{\varepsilon_{1}},\ldots,X^{\varepsilon_{n}}
in
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}

by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}=gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}&\qquad\hbox{and}&Y^{\varepsilon_{j+1}^{\vee}}=T_{j}^{-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{j}^{\vee}}T_{j}^{-1},\\
X^{\varepsilon_{1}}=g^{\vee}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1},&\quad\hbox{and}&X^{\varepsilon_{j+1}}=T_{j}X^{\varepsilon_{j}}T_{j},\end{array}

(5.3)

for
j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
. If
\iota\colon\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}

and
\eta\colon\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
are the
involutions in Theorem
5.1
then

\iota(X^{\varepsilon_{i}})=Y^{\varepsilon_{i}}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\eta(X^{\varepsilon_{i}})=X^{-\varepsilon_{n-i+1}}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\eta(Y^{\varepsilon_{i}})=Y^{-\varepsilon_{n-i+1}},

(5.4)

for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.

The subgroup generated by
g^{\vee},T_{1},\ldots,T_{n-1}
has a pictorial representation given by

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{matrix}\quad\hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$.}}

(5.5)

Use the notation

X_{i}=X^{\varepsilon_{i}}
,

\hbox{and let}\qquad X^{\mu}=X_{1}^{\mu_{1}}\cdots X_{n}^{\mu_{n}}=X^{\mu_{1}\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\mu_{n}\varepsilon_{n}},\qquad\hbox{for $\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}$.}

Similarly use the notation

Y_{i}=Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{i}}
,

\hbox{and let}\qquad Y^{\lambda^{\vee}}=Y_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots X_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}=Y^{\lambda_{1}\varepsilon^{\vee}_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{n}\varepsilon^{\vee}_{n}},\qquad\hbox{for $\lambda^{\vee}=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}$.}

The pictorial representation provides an easy check of the relations

\displaystyle(g^{\vee})^{n}=X^{\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{n}}\quad\hbox{and}\quad X^{\varepsilon_{i}}X^{\varepsilon_{j}}=X^{\varepsilon_{j}}X^{\varepsilon_{i}},

(5.6)

and similarly,

\displaystyle g^{n}=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}\quad\hbox{and}\quad Y^{\varepsilon_{i}^{\vee}}Y^{\varepsilon_{j}^{\vee}}=Y^{\varepsilon_{j}^{\vee}}Y^{\varepsilon_{i}^{\vee}},

(5.7)

for
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. The pictorial perspective also verifies the relations

S_{0}T_{s_{\varphi}}=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}-\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}\quad\hbox{and}\quad(S_{0}^{\vee})^{-1}T^{-1}_{s_{\varphi}}=X^{\varepsilon_{1}-\varepsilon_{n}},\quad\hbox{where}\quad T_{s_{\varphi}}=T_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}\cdots T_{n-1}.

(5.8)

Theorem 5.2
.

The group
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
is presented by generators
q,g^{\vee},S_{0}^{\vee},T_{1},\ldots,T_{n-1}
and

Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}},\ldots,Y^{\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}
and relations

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{matrix}\quad g^{\vee}T_{i}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=T_{i+1},\quad g^{\vee}T_{n-1}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=S_{0}^{\vee},

q\in Z(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}),\qquad Y^{\varepsilon_{k}^{\vee}}Y^{\varepsilon_{j}^{\vee}}=Y^{\varepsilon_{j}^{\vee}}Y^{\varepsilon_{k}^{\vee}}\ \hbox{ for $k,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$,}

(5.9)

Y^{\varepsilon_{i+1}^{\vee}}=T_{i}^{-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{i}^{\vee}}T_{i}^{-1},\quad\hbox{and}\quad T_{i}Y^{\varepsilon_{j}^{\vee}}=Y^{\varepsilon_{j}^{\vee}}T_{i}\ \hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$ and $j\neq i,i+1$,}

(5.10)

g^{\vee}Y^{\varepsilon_{i}^{\vee}}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=Y^{\varepsilon_{i+1}^{\vee}}\ \hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$,}\quad\hbox{and}\quad g^{\vee}Y^{\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=qY^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}},

(5.11)

The group
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
is presented by generators

q,g,T_{0},T_{1},\ldots,T_{n-1}
and
X^{\varepsilon_{1}},\ldots,X^{\varepsilon_{n}}

and relations

}{{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{{}}}{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}

{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}}{{}}{{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{{}}}{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}
}{{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{{}}}{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}
}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{}{}{}{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 41.91 2.25 C 65.77 23.41 83.54 32.3 114.79 38.69 M 121.43 38.69 C 152.68 32.3 170.45 23.41 194.31 2.25}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}{{}}
{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 82.13 0 L 154.09 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{matrix}\quad gT_{i}g^{-1}=T_{i+1},\quad gT_{n-1}g^{-1}=S_{0},

q\in Z(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}),\qquad X^{\varepsilon_{k}}X^{\varepsilon_{j}}=X^{\varepsilon_{j}}X^{\varepsilon_{k}}\ \hbox{for $k,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$,}

(5.12)

X^{\varepsilon_{i+1}}=T_{i}X^{\varepsilon_{i}}T_{i},\quad\hbox{and}\quad T_{i}X^{\varepsilon_{j}}=X^{\varepsilon_{j}}T_{i},\ \hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$ and $j\neq i,i+1$,}

(5.13)

gX^{\varepsilon_{i}}g^{-1}=X^{\varepsilon_{i+1}},\ \ \hbox{for $i\in\{1,2,\ldots,n-1\}$\hskip 10.22217ptand}\qquad gX^{\varepsilon_{n}}g^{-1}=q^{-1}X^{\varepsilon_{1}}.

(5.14)

Proof.

The proof is by showing that the relations in (
5.9
), (
5.10
)
and (
5.11
) follow from the defining relations of
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
, and vice versa.

(
5.1
)&(
5.2
)

\Longrightarrow
(a): Using (
5.3
) to
define
Y^{\varepsilon_{i}^{\vee}}
, the pictorial perspective
establishes the relations in
(
5.9
) and (
5.10
). The proof of the relations in (
5.11
) is completed by

\displaystyle g^{\vee}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}

\displaystyle=g^{\vee}gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}=T_{1}^{-1}gg^{\vee}T_{n-2}\cdots T_{1}=T_{1}^{-1}gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{2}g^{\vee}

\displaystyle=T_{1}^{-1}gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{2}T_{1}T_{1}^{-1}g^{\vee}=T_{1}^{-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}g^{\vee}=Y^{\varepsilon_{2}^{\vee}}g^{\vee},

\displaystyle g^{\vee}Y^{\varepsilon_{i}^{\vee}}(g^{\vee})^{-1}

\displaystyle=g^{\vee}T_{i-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{i-1}^{-1}(g^{\vee})^{-1}

\displaystyle=T_{i}^{-1}\cdots T_{2}^{-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{2}^{\vee}}T_{2}^{-1}\cdots T_{i}^{-1}=Y^{\varepsilon_{i+1}^{\vee}},\qquad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle g^{\vee}Y^{\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}(g^{\vee})^{-1}

\displaystyle=g^{\vee}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}(g^{\vee})^{-1}

\displaystyle=g^{\vee}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=g^{\vee}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}g(g^{\vee})^{-1}

\displaystyle=qg^{\vee}(g^{\vee})^{-1}gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}=qY^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}.

(a)
\Rightarrow
(
5.1
)&(
5.2
):
Use (
5.8
) to define
g
and
S_{0}
in terms of
T_{i}
and
Y^{\varepsilon_{i}^{\vee}}
s.
If
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-2\}
then

\displaystyle gT_{i}g^{-1}

\displaystyle=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}T_{i}T_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}Y^{-\varepsilon^{\vee}_{1}}=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{i+1}^{-1}T_{i}T_{i+1}\cdots T_{1}Y^{-\varepsilon^{\vee}_{1}}

\displaystyle=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T^{-1}_{i}T_{i}T_{i+1}T_{i}^{-1}T_{i}\cdots T_{1}Y^{-\varepsilon^{\vee}_{1}}=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T^{-1}_{i-1}T_{i+1}T_{i-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}Y^{-\varepsilon^{\vee}_{1}}

\displaystyle=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{i+1}Y^{-\varepsilon^{\vee}_{1}}=T_{i+1},

and

\displaystyle gT_{n-1}g^{-1}

\displaystyle=gT_{n-1}T_{n-2}\cdots T_{1}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-2}^{-1}g^{-1}=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-2}^{-1}g^{-1}

\displaystyle=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}g^{-1}T_{2}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}g^{-1}T_{1}\cdots T_{n-1}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}T_{2}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}

\displaystyle=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}-\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}=S_{0},

and

\displaystyle gS_{0}g^{-1}

\displaystyle=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}-\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}T_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}Y^{-\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}

\displaystyle=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n}^{-1}Y^{-\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{2}^{-1}Y^{-\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}

\displaystyle=Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}T_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}Y^{-\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}\cdots T_{n-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}Y^{-\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{2}^{-1}=T_{1},

which establishes the relations in (
5.1
).

To prove the first relation in (
5.2
):

\displaystyle T_{1}g^{\vee}g

\displaystyle=T_{1}g^{\vee}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}=T_{1}Y^{\varepsilon_{2}^{\vee}}g^{\vee}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}

\displaystyle=T_{1}T_{1}^{-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}g^{\vee}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}=gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{2}T_{1}T_{1}^{-1}g^{\vee}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}

\displaystyle=gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{2}g^{\vee}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}=gg^{\vee}T_{n-2}\cdots T_{1}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}=gg^{\vee}T_{n-1}^{-1},

and to prove the second relation in (
5.2
):

\displaystyle T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}

\displaystyle g(g^{\vee})^{-1}=T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}(g^{\vee})^{-1}

\displaystyle=T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1}Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{n-1}^{-1}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=Y^{\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}(g^{\vee})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(g^{\vee})^{-1}g^{\vee}Y^{\varepsilon_{n}^{\vee}}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=(g^{\vee})^{-1}qY^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}=q(g^{\vee})^{-1}gT_{n-1}\cdots T_{1}.

Part (b) follows from part (a) by applying the duality involution
\iota
.
∎

5.3
The elements
X^{t_{\mu}w}

Use the notation

X_{i}=X^{\varepsilon_{i}}
,

\hbox{and let}\qquad X^{\mu}=X_{1}^{\mu_{1}}\cdots X_{n}^{\mu_{n}}=X^{\mu_{1}\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\mu_{n}\varepsilon_{n}},\qquad\hbox{for $\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}$.}

Using the notation of the affine Weyl group
W=\{t_{\mu}w\ |\ \mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},w\in S_{n}\}
from
Section
2.1
,
for
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
w\in S_{n}
define

X^{t_{\mu}w}=X^{\mu}T_{w},\qquad\hbox{where $T_{w}=T_{i_{1}}\cdots T_{i_{\ell}}$
if $w=s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{\ell}}$ is a reduced word.}

(5.15)

The following Proposition establishes how these elements are affected by right multiplication
by the generators
T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}
and
g^{\vee}
of
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
w\in S_{n}
. Then

X^{\mu}T_{ws_{i}}=\begin{cases}X^{\mu}T_{w}T_{i},&\hbox{if $\ell(ws_{i})>\ell(w)$,}\\
X^{\mu}T_{w}T_{i}^{-1},&\hbox{if $\ell(ws_{i})<\ell(w)$,}\end{cases}\quad\hbox{and}\quad X^{\mu}T_{w}g^{\vee}=X^{\mu}X_{w(1)}T_{ws_{1}\cdots s_{n-1}}.

(5.16)

Proof.

The first equality follows from the fact that if
z\in S_{n}
and
\ell(zs_{i})>\ell(z)

then
T_{z}T_{i}=T_{zs_{i}}
. For the second equality:
Let
k=w(1)
and write
w=s_{k-1}\cdots s_{1}z
with
z
in the subgroup of
S_{n}

that is generated by
s_{2},\ldots,s_{n-1}
.
Letting
c_{n}=s_{1}\cdots s_{n-1}
and using

g^{\vee}T_{i}(g^{\vee})^{-1}=T_{i+1}
then

(g^{\vee})^{-1}T_{z}g^{\vee}=T_{c_{n}^{-1}zc_{n}}
and

\displaystyle T_{w}g^{\vee}

\displaystyle=T_{k-1}\cdots T_{1}T_{z}g^{\vee}=T_{k-1}\cdots T_{1}g^{\vee}((g^{\vee})^{-1}T_{z}g^{\vee})

\displaystyle=T_{k-1}\cdots T_{1}g^{\vee}T_{c_{n}^{-1}zc_{n}}=(T_{k-1}\cdots T_{1}g^{\vee}T_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{k}^{-1})T_{k}\cdots T_{n-1}T_{c_{n}^{-1}zc_{n}}

\displaystyle=X_{k}T_{k}\cdots T_{n-1}T_{s_{k}\cdots s_{n-1}c_{n}^{-1}zc_{n}}=X_{k}T_{s_{k-1}\cdots s_{1}zc_{n}}=X_{k}T_{wc_{n}}.

∎

5.4
The type
GL_{n}
double affine Hecke algebra (DAHA)

The
type
GL_{n}
double affine Hecke algebra

\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}
is the quotient of the
group algebra of
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}
by the relations

(T_{i}-t^{\frac{1}{2}})(T_{i}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}})=0,\qquad\hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$.}

(5.17)

The involutions

\iota\colon\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}

and
\eta\colon\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{GL_{n}}

from Theorem
5.1
preserve the relations in
(
5.17
) to provide involutions

\iota\colon\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}\to\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\eta\colon\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}\to\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}.

(5.18)

The following proposition explains how
g,T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}
move past the
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}

inside the affine Hecke algebra.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
define
X^{\mu}=X^{\mu_{1}\varepsilon_{1}+\cdots+\mu_{n}\varepsilon_{n}}
.
The symmetric group
S_{n}
acts on
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
by permuting coordinates.
Let
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n-1}
be the simple reflections in
S_{n}
.
Then, as elements of
\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}
,

gX^{\mu}=q^{-\mu_{n}}X^{s_{1}s_{2}\cdots s_{n-1}\mu}g=q^{-\mu_{n}}X^{(\mu_{n},\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n-1})}g,

and

T_{i}X^{\mu}=(s_{i}X^{\mu})T_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X^{\mu},\quad\hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$}.

Proof.

Start with
X_{i+1}=T_{i}X_{i}T_{i}
and use
T_{i}^{-1}=T_{i}-(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})
to get

\displaystyle T_{i}X_{i}

\displaystyle=X_{i+1}T_{i}^{-1}=X_{i+1}(T_{i}-(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}))=X^{s_{i}\varepsilon_{i}}T_{i}+(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})\frac{X_{i}-X_{i+1}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=X^{s_{i}\varepsilon_{i}}T_{i}+\frac{(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X_{i}.

and

\displaystyle T_{i}X_{i+1}

\displaystyle=T_{i}^{2}X_{i}T_{i}=((t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})T_{i}+1)X_{i}T_{i}=X_{i}T_{i}+(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})X_{i+1}

\displaystyle=X^{s_{i}\varepsilon_{i+1}}T_{i}+(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})\frac{X_{i+1}-X_{i}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=(s_{i}X_{i+1})T_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X_{i+1}

and, for
j\not\in\{i,i+1\}
,

\displaystyle{T_{i}X_{j}=X_{j}T_{i}=(s_{i}X_{j})T_{i}+0=(s_{i}X_{j})T_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X_{j}.}

If

T_{i}X^{\mu}=(s_{i}X^{\mu})T_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X^{\mu}\quad\hbox{and}\quad T_{i}X^{\nu}=(s_{i}X^{\nu})T_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X^{\nu}

then

\displaystyle T_{i}X^{\mu+\nu}

\displaystyle=T_{i}X^{\mu}X^{\nu}=\left((s_{i}X^{\mu})T_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X^{\mu}\right)X^{\nu}

\displaystyle=(s_{i}X^{\mu})\left((s_{i}X^{\nu})T_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X^{\nu}\right)+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(X^{\mu}-(s_{i}X^{\mu}))X^{\nu}

\displaystyle=(s_{i}X^{\mu+\nu})T_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(X^{s_{i}\mu+\nu}-X^{s_{i}(\mu+\nu)}+X^{\mu+\nu}-X^{s_{i}\mu+\nu})

\displaystyle=(s_{i}X^{\mu+\nu})T_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X^{\mu+\nu}.

Since
gX_{n}g^{-1}=q^{-1}X_{1}
and
gX_{i}g^{-1}=X_{i+1}
then

gX^{(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})}=gX_{1}^{\mu_{1}}\cdots X_{n}^{\mu_{n}}=X_{2}^{\mu_{1}}\cdots X_{n}^{\mu_{n-1}}gX_{n}^{\mu_{n}}=q^{-\mu_{n}}X_{2}^{\mu_{1}}\cdots X_{n}^{\mu_{n-1}}X_{1}^{\mu_{n}}g.

∎

5.5
Intertwiners

Structurally, the elements
X^{\varepsilon_{1}},\ldots,X^{\varepsilon_{n}}
are playing the
role of generators of a polynomial ring inside of the double affine Hecke algebra
\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}
.
The next key point is that we can produce elements
\tau^{\vee}_{1},\ldots,\tau^{\vee}_{n-1}

and
\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}

which are “replacements” for the generators
T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}
and
g^{\vee}
,
and which move past the elements
Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{1}},\ldots,Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{n}}
in the best possible way, by permuting the
Y_{i}
, as seen in
(
5.24
).

Define
Y_{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}
by setting

Y_{i}=Y^{\varepsilon_{i}^{\vee}}\ \ \hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad Y_{j+n}=qY_{j}\ \ \hbox{for $j\in\mathbb{Z}$.}

(5.19)

Letting

Y^{K}=q^{-1}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\varepsilon^{\vee}_{0}=\varepsilon^{\vee}_{n}+K\quad\hbox{then}\quad Y_{0}=Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{0}}=Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{n}+K}=Y^{K}Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{n}}=q^{-1}Y_{n}.

(5.20)

Let

\tau_{\pi}^{\vee}=g^{\vee}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\tau_{i}^{\vee}=T_{i}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}\quad\hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$.}

(5.21)

For
w\in W
define

\tau_{w}^{\vee}=\tau_{i_{1}}^{\vee}\cdots\tau_{i_{\ell}}^{\vee}\qquad\hbox{for a reduced word $w=s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{\ell}}$.}

The following proposition establishes that the
\tau^{\vee}_{i}
satisfy the braid relations
so that the element
\tau^{\vee}_{w}
does not depend on the choice of reduced word for

w
. The
\tau^{\vee}_{i}
do not quite generate a symmetric group, because
(\tau^{\vee}_{i})^{2}

is not the identity.

Proposition 5.5
.

For
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-2\}
and
j,k\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
with
k\not\in\{j+1,j-1\}
,

\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}\tau_{i}^{\vee}=\tau_{i+1}^{\vee}\tau^{\vee}_{\pi},\qquad\tau_{i}^{\vee}\tau^{\vee}_{i+1}\tau_{i}^{\vee}=\tau_{i+1}^{\vee}\tau^{\vee}_{i}\tau_{i+1}^{\vee}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\tau_{k}^{\vee}\tau_{j}^{\vee}=\tau_{j}^{\vee}\tau_{k}^{\vee};

(5.22)

(t^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{i}^{\vee})^{2}=\frac{(1-tY_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1})(1-tY_{i}Y_{i+1}^{-1})}{(1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1})(1-Y_{i}Y_{i+1}^{-1})},\qquad\hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$;}

(5.23)

Y_{i}\tau_{w}^{\vee}=\tau_{w}^{\vee}Y_{w^{-1}(i)}\qquad\hbox{for $w\in W$ and $i\in\mathbb{Z}$.}

(5.24)

Proof.

Using
T_{i}=T_{i}^{-1}+(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})
,

\displaystyle\tau_{i}^{\vee}

\displaystyle=T_{i}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}=(T_{i}^{-1}+(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}

\displaystyle=T_{i}^{-1}+\frac{(Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}-1+1)t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}=T_{i}^{-1}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}.

(5.25)

To prove (
5.24
), prove that

Y_{1}\tau_{\pi}^{\vee}=q^{-1}\tau_{\pi}^{\vee}Y_{n}\quad\hbox{and}\quad Y_{i}\tau_{\pi}^{\vee}=\tau_{\pi}^{\vee}Y_{i-1}\quad\hbox{for $i\in\{2,\ldots n\},\quad and$}

(5.26)

Y_{i}\tau_{i}^{\vee}=\tau_{i}^{\vee}Y_{i+1},\qquad Y_{i+1}\tau_{i}^{\vee}=\tau_{i}^{\vee}Y_{i}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad Y_{k}\tau_{i}^{\vee}=\tau_{i}^{\vee}Y_{k},

for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
and
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
k\not\in\{i,i+1\}
.
By (
5.11
) and (
2.2
),

\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{n}}=qY^{\varepsilon_{1}}\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}\quad\hbox{gives}\quad Y_{1}\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}=\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}q^{-1}Y_{n}=\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}Y_{0}=\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}Y_{\pi^{-1}(1)}.

and
Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{i+1}}g^{\vee}=g^{\vee}Y^{\varepsilon^{\vee}_{i}}
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
.
Using
Y_{i+1}=T_{i}^{-1}Y_{i}T_{i}^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\tau_{i}^{\vee}Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\left(T_{i}^{-1}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}\right)Y_{i}=\left(Y_{i+1}T_{i}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)Y_{i+1}}{1-Y_{i+1}}\right)=Y_{i+1}\tau^{\vee}_{i},\quad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle\tau_{i}^{\vee}Y_{i+1}

\displaystyle=\left(T_{i}Y_{i+1}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)Y_{i+1}}{1-Y_{i+1}}\right)=Y_{i}\left(T_{i}^{-1}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}\right)=Y_{i}\tau_{i}^{\vee}.

If
k\not\in\{i,i+1\}
then
T_{i}Y_{k}=Y_{k}T_{i}
and
Y_{i}Y_{k}=Y_{k}Y_{i}
and
Y_{i+1}Y_{k}=Y_{k}Y_{i+1}

and so

\tau_{i}^{\vee}Y_{k}=\left(T_{i}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}\right)Y_{k}=Y_{k}\left(T_{i}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}\right)=Y_{k}\tau_{i}^{\vee}.

Using (
5.25
),

\displaystyle(\tau_{i}^{\vee})^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(T_{i}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}\right)\tau_{i}^{\vee}=T_{i}\tau_{i}^{\vee}+\tau_{i}^{\vee}\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i+1}^{-1}Y_{i}}

\displaystyle=T_{i}\left(T_{i}^{-1}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}\right)+\left(T_{i}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}\right)\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i+1}^{-1}Y_{i}}

\displaystyle=1+T_{i}\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}+T_{i}\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i+1}^{-1}Y_{i}}+\left(\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1}}\right)\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-t)}{1-Y_{i+1}^{-1}Y_{i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1})(1-Y_{i+1}^{-1}Y_{i})+t^{-1}-2+t}{(1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1})(1-Y_{i+1}^{-1}Y_{i})}=\frac{(t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1})(t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{i}Y_{i+1}^{-1})}{(1-Y_{i}^{-1}Y_{i+1})(1-Y_{i}Y_{i+1}^{-1})}.

The proof of the relations in (
5.22
) can be done by comparing
the brute force expansion of each side using the relations in (
5.10
)
and (
5.11
). An alternative, often used, argument is to note that the action of each
side on the polynomial representation (which is a faithful representation of
\widetilde{H}
)
produces the same output (see Proposition 2.14(e) in
[
Ra03
]
).
∎

5.6
The polynomial representation

In this section we build the action of the double affine Hecke algebra on Laurent
polynomials in
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
. The elements
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
are then
a large family of commuting elements acting on the polynomial ring

\mathbb{C}[X_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,X_{n}^{\pm 1}]
. The Macdonald polynomials are the
simultaneous eigenvectors for the family of commuting elements
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
.

Let
q,t\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
such that
1\not\in\{q^{a}t^{b}\ |\ \hbox{$a,b\in\mathbb{Z}$ and
$a$ and $b$ not both $0$}\}
.
The polynomial representation is

\mathbb{C}[X]=\mathbb{C}[X_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,X_{n}^{\pm 1}]=\hbox{$\mathbb{C}$-span}\{X^{\mu}\mathbf{1}\ |\ \mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\}

with the action of DAHA determined by

T_{i}\mathbf{1}=t^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}
and

g\mathbf{1}=\mathbf{1}

so that, by (
5.3
),

Y^{\varepsilon_{1}^{\vee}}\mathbf{1}=t^{\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\mathbf{1}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad Y^{\varepsilon_{i}}\mathbf{1}=t^{\frac{1}{2}(n-1)-2(i-1)\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}=t^{-(i-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\mathbf{1}.

(5.27)

Following the notation of
[
Mac
, Ch. VI (3.1)]
,
let
T_{q^{-1},X_{n}}
be the operator on
\mathbb{C}[X_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,X_{n}^{\pm 1}]
given by

(T_{q^{-1},X_{n}}h)(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})=h(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n-1},q^{-1}X_{n}).

Proposition 5.6
.

As operators on the polynomial representation

g=s_{1}s_{2}\cdots s_{n-1}T_{q^{-1},X_{n}}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad T_{i}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(t-\frac{tX_{i}-X_{i+1}}{X_{i}-X_{i+1}}(1-s_{i})\big)\qquad\hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$.}

Proof.

The first statement in Proposition
5.4
gives

gX^{\mu}\mathbf{1}=(s_{1}s_{2}\cdots s_{n-1}T_{q^{-1}X_{n}}X^{\mu})\mathbf{1},

since
T_{q^{-1},X_{n}}X^{\mu}=q^{-\mu_{n}}X^{\mu}
.
Using the second statement in Proposition
5.4
,

\displaystyle T_{i}X^{\mu}\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=\Big((s_{i}X^{\mu})t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})X^{\mu}\Big)\mathbf{1}

(5.28)

\displaystyle=\left(t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-s_{i})+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(1-s_{i})\right)X^{\mu}\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=\left(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{1-X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}}(X_{i}X_{i+1}^{-1}t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}})(1-s_{i})\right)X^{\mu}\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=\left(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{X_{i}-X_{i+1}}(-X_{i}t^{\frac{1}{2}}+X_{i+1}t^{-\frac{1}{2}})(1-s_{i})\right)X^{\mu}\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(t-\frac{tX_{i}-X_{i+1}}{X_{i}-X_{i+1}}(1-s_{i})\right)X^{\mu}\mathbf{1}.

∎

5.7
Constructing the nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomials
E_{\mu}

The Macdonald polynomials are the
simultaneous eigenvectors for the action of
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}

on the polynomial ring
\mathbb{C}[X_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,X_{n}^{\pm 1}]
. Because the
intertwiners
\tau^{\vee}_{1},\ldots,\tau^{\vee}_{n-1},\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}

move past
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
in the best possible way, they are the
perfect tools for physically constructing the Macdonald polynomials
E_{\mu}
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}
and let
u_{\mu}
and
v_{\mu}
be as
in (
2.15
) and let

\ell(v_{\mu})
be the number of inversions of
v_{\mu}
.
Choose a reduced word
\vec{u}_{\mu}=s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{\ell}}
(where

i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}\in\{\pi,1,\ldots,n-1\}
and let

\tau_{u_{\mu}}^{\vee}=\tau_{i_{1}}^{\vee}\cdots\tau_{i_{\ell}}^{\vee}
.
Define

E_{\mu}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau_{u_{\mu}}^{\vee}\mathbf{1}.\qquad\hbox{Then}\quad Y_{i}E_{\mu}=q^{-\mu_{i}}t^{-(v_{\mu}(i)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}E_{\mu},

for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, and the coefficient of
x^{\mu}
in
E_{\mu}
is 1.

Proof.

Compute the eigenvalue as follows:

\displaystyle Y_{i}E_{\mu}

\displaystyle=Y_{i}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau_{u_{\mu}}^{\vee}\mathbf{1}\qquad\hbox{(by definition of $E_{\mu}$)}

\displaystyle=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau_{u_{\mu}}^{\vee}Y_{u_{\mu}^{-1}(i)}\mathbf{1}\qquad\hbox{(by \eqref{Ypasttau})}

\displaystyle=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau_{u_{\mu}}^{\vee}Y_{v_{\mu}t_{\mu}^{-1}(i)}\mathbf{1}\qquad\hbox{(by \eqref{umudefn})}

\displaystyle=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau_{u_{\mu}}^{\vee}Y_{v_{\mu}(i-n\mu_{i})}\mathbf{1}\qquad\hbox{(by \eqref{tmudefn})}

\displaystyle=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau_{u_{\mu}}^{\vee}Y_{v_{\mu}(i)-n\mu_{i}}\mathbf{1}\qquad\hbox{(by \eqref{nperiodicdefn})}

\displaystyle=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau_{u_{\mu}}^{\vee}q^{-\mu_{i}}Y_{v_{\mu}(i)}\mathbf{1}\qquad\hbox{(by \eqref{YiforiinZ})}

\displaystyle=q^{-\mu_{i}}t^{-(v_{\mu}(i)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}(t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau_{u_{\mu}}^{\vee}\mathbf{1})\qquad\hbox{(by \eqref{Yion1})}

\displaystyle=q^{-\mu_{i}}t^{-(v_{\mu}(i)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}E_{\mu}\qquad\hbox{(by definition of $E_{\mu}$).}

Using (
5.15
)
and (
5.3
),
the top term of the expansion of in
t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v_{\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}

is

t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}X^{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}X^{t_{\mu}v^{-1}_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}X^{\mu}T_{v^{-1}_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}X^{\mu}t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\mathbf{1}=X^{\mu}\mathbf{1}=x^{\mu}.

∎

5.8
Steps and symmetries of
E_{\mu}

The following Proposition establishes the inductive construction of the
E_{\mu}

and the symmetries in (
3.3
).
For examples of the
E_{\mu}
see Proposition
3.5
, which provides explicit formulas
for the cases when
\mu
has 1 or 2 boxes.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
.

If
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
and
\mu_{i}>\mu_{i+1}
then

E_{s_{i}\mu}=t^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{i}^{\vee}E_{\mu}
.

E_{(\mu_{n}+1,\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n-1})}=t^{\#\{i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\ |\ \mu_{i}>\mu_{n}\}-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\tau_{\pi}^{\vee}E_{(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})}
.

E_{(\mu_{n}+1,\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n-1})}=q^{\mu_{n}}x_{1}E_{\mu}(x_{2},\ldots,x_{n},q^{-1}x_{1})
.

E_{(\mu_{1}+1,\ldots,\mu_{n}+1)}=x_{1}\cdots x_{n}E_{(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})}
.

E_{(-\mu_{n},\ldots,-\mu_{1})}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q,t)=E_{\mu}(x^{-1}_{n},\ldots,x^{-1}_{1};q,t)
.

Proof.

(a) Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
and let
i
be such that
\mu_{i}>\mu_{i+1}
. By Proposition
2.1
(e),
\ell(v_{s_{i}\mu})-\ell(v_{\mu})=-1
, giving

E_{s_{i}\mu}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{s_{i}\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{s_{i}\mu}}\mathbf{1}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{s_{i}\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{i}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}(\ell(v^{-1}_{s_{i}\mu})-\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu}))}\tau^{\vee}_{i}E_{\mu}=(t^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{i}^{\vee})E_{\mu}.

(b) The left hand side is
E_{\pi\mu}
and

\displaystyle E_{\pi\mu}

\displaystyle=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\pi\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\pi\mu}}\mathbf{1}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\pi\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}(\ell(v^{-1}_{\pi\mu})-\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu}))}\tau^{\vee}_{\pi}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}=t^{-(v_{\mu}(n)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\tau_{\pi}^{\vee}E_{\mu}.

The result then follows from Proposition
2.1
(b).

(c)
The second relation in (
5.2
)
and the second relation in (
5.11
) give
X_{1}g=g^{\vee}Y_{n}
.
Beginning with the right hand side of (b) and using
g^{\vee}Y_{n}=X_{1}g
gives

\displaystyle t^{-(v_{\mu}(n)-1+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\tau_{\pi}^{\vee}E_{\mu}=t^{-(v_{\mu}(n)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}g^{\vee}E_{\mu}=g^{\vee}q^{\mu_{n}}Y_{n}E_{\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad=q^{\mu_{n}}x_{1}gE_{\mu}=q^{\mu_{n}}x_{1}s_{1}\cdots s_{n-1}T_{q^{-1},x_{n}}E_{\mu}=q^{\mu_{n}}x_{1}E_{\mu}(x_{2},\ldots,x_{n},q^{-1}x_{1}).

(d) By (
5.6
),
(\tau^{\vee}_{\pi})^{n}=(g^{\vee})^{n}=X_{1}\cdots X_{n}
and so

E_{(\mu_{1}+1,\ldots,\mu_{n}+1)}=(\tau^{\vee}_{\pi})^{n}E_{(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})}=x_{1}\cdots x_{n}E_{(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})}.

(e) Let
\eta\colon\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}\to\tilde{H}_{GL_{n}}
be the involution in
(
5.18
).
Let
w_{0}
be the longest element of

S_{n}
so that
w_{0}(i)=n-i+1
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
Using the last relation in (
5.4
),

\displaystyle Y_{i}\eta(E_{\mu}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=\eta(Y^{-1}_{n-i+1}E_{\mu}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n}))\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=q^{\mu_{n-i+1}}t^{(v_{\mu}(n-i+1)-1)-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\eta(E_{\mu}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=q^{\mu_{n-i+1}}t^{(w_{0}v_{-w_{0}\mu}w_{0}(n-i+1)-1)-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\eta(E_{\mu}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\mathbf{1},

\displaystyle=q^{-(-w_{0}\mu)_{i}}t^{(w_{0}v_{-w_{0}\mu}(i)-1)-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\eta(E_{\mu}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\mathbf{1},

\displaystyle=q^{-(-w_{0}\mu)_{i}}t^{(n-v_{-w_{0}\mu}(i)+1)-1-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\eta(E_{\mu}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\mathbf{1},

\displaystyle=q^{-(-w_{0}\mu)_{i}}t^{-(v_{-w_{0}\mu}(i)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}\eta(E_{\mu}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\mathbf{1},

so that
\eta(E_{\mu}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}))\mathbf{1}=E_{\mu}(x_{n}^{-1},\ldots,x_{1}^{-1})

satisfies the conditions (from Theorem
5.7
) determining
E_{-w_{0}\mu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
∎
